To this end, they pick and choose the evidences that corroborate their misguidance, despite being weak or abrogated."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Yaqoubi201537-362]&#x20;

Academics [Robyn Creswell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robyn_Creswell) and [Bernard Haykel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Haykel) of *[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker)* have criticised ISIL's execution of Muslims for breach of traditional *[sharia](28840.md)* law while violating it simultaneously themselves (encouraging women to emigrate to its territory, travelling without a *[Wali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wali)*—male guardian—and in violation of his wishes).[^cite_note-363] as well as its love of archaic imagery (horsemen and swords) while engaging in *[bid'ah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bid%27ah)* (religious innovation) in establishing female religious police (known as [Al-Khansaa Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Khansaa_Brigade)).[^cite_note-364]&#x20;

Two days after the beheading of [Hervé Gourdel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herv%C3%A9_Gourdel), hundreds of Muslims gathered in the [Grand Mosque of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Mosque_of_Paris) to show solidarity against the beheading. The protest was led by the leader of the [French Council of the Muslim Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Council_of_the_Muslim_Faith), [Dalil Boubakeur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalil_Boubakeur), and was joined by thousands of other Muslims around the country under the slogan "Not in my name".[^cite_note-ibt-09-28-365] French president [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande) said Gourdel's beheading was "cowardly" and "cruel", and confirmed that airstrikes would continue against ISIL in Iraq. Hollande also called for three days of national mourning, with flags flown at half-mast throughout the country and said that security would be increased throughout Paris.[^cite_note-ibt-09-28-365]&#x20;

#### &#x20;Other jihadist groups

According to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, "All of the most influential jihadist theorists are criticising the Islamic State as deviant, calling its self-proclaimed caliphate null and void" and they have denounced it for its beheadings of journalists[^cite_note-366] and aid workers.[^cite_note-ISIS_NYT-184] ISIL is widely denounced by a broad range of Islamic clerics, including Saudi and al-Qaeda-orientated clerics.[^cite_note-HuffPost_Wahhabi-34][^cite_note-ISIS_NYT-184] [Muhammad al-Yaqoubi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_al-Yaqoubi) states, "It is enough of a proof of the extreme ideology of ISIS that the top leaders of Salafi-Jihadism have disclaimed it."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Yaqoubi201519-367] Other critics of ISIL's brand of Sunni Islam include Salafists who previously publicly supported jihadist groups such as al-Qaeda: for example, the Saudi government official [Saleh Al-Fawzan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saleh_Al-Fawzan), known for his extremist views, who claims that ISIL is a creation of "Zionists, Crusaders and Safavids", and the Jordanian-Palestinian writer [Abu Muhammad al-Maqdisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Muhammad_al-Maqdisi), the former spiritual mentor to [Abu Musab al-Zarqawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Musab_al-Zarqawi), who was released from prison in Jordan in June 2014 and accused ISIL of driving a wedge between Muslims.[^cite_note-econ140906-345]&#x20;

An [Islamic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Front_\(Syria\)) [sharia](28840.md) court judge in Aleppo, Mohamed Najeeb Bannan, stated: "The legal reference is the Islamic Sharia. The cases are different, from robberies to drug use, to moral crimes. It's our duty to look at any crime that comes to us... After the regime has fallen, we believe that the Muslim majority in Syria will ask for an Islamic state. Of course, it's very important to point out that some say the Islamic Sharia will cut off people's hands and heads, but it only applies to criminals. And to start off by killing, crucifying etc. That is not correct at all." In response to being asked what the difference between the Islamic Front's and ISIL's version of sharia would be, he said, "One of their mistakes is before the regime has fallen, and before they've established what in Sharia is called Tamkeen \[having a stable state], they started applying Sharia, thinking God gave them permission to control the land and establish a Caliphate. This goes against the beliefs of religious scholars around the world. This is what \[IS] did wrong. This is going to cause a lot of trouble. Anyone who opposes \[IS] will be considered against Sharia and will be severely punished."[^cite_note-368]&#x20;

Al-Qaeda and al-Nusra have been trying to take advantage of ISIL's rise, by attempting to present themselves as "moderate" compared to "extremist" ISIL, although they have the same aim of establishing sharia and a caliphate, but doing so in a more gradual manner.[^cite_note-369] Al-Nusra has criticised the way in which ISIL fully and immediately institutes sharia in the areas that fall under its control, since it alienates people too much. It supports the gradual, slower approach favoured by al-Qaeda, preparing society to accept sharia and indoctrinating people through education before implementing the [hudud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudud) aspects in sharia, which they believe supports punishments such as throwing homosexuals from the top of buildings, chopping limbs off, and public stoning.[^cite_note-Joscelyn_2015-09-197] Al-Nusra and ISIL are both hostile towards the [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze). However, while al-Nusra has typically destroyed Druze shrines and pressured them to convert to Sunni Islam, ISIL regards the entire Druze community as a valid target for violence, as it does the [Yazidis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazidis).[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-371]&#x20;

In February 2014, [Ayman al-Zawahiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayman_al-Zawahiri), the leader of Al-Qaeda, announced that his group [Al-Qaeda](1921.md) had cut ties with the Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant and denounced ISIL after being unable to reconcile a conflict between them and the al-Qaeda affiliate [al-Nusra Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nusra_Front).[^cite_note-372]&#x20;

In September 2015, Ayman al-Zawahiri called for consultation ([shura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shura)) within the "prophetic method" to be used when establishing the caliphate, criticising al-Baghdadi for not following the required steps. Al-Zawahiri called upon ISIL members to close ranks and join al-Qaeda in fighting against [Assad](364813.md), the [Shia](26961.md), Russia, Europe, and America and to stop the infighting between jihadist groups. He called upon jihadists to establish Islamic entities in Egypt and the Levant, slowly implementing sharia before establishing a caliphate, and has called for violent assaults against America and the West.[^cite_note-373]&#x20;

The [Jaysh al-Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaysh_al-Islam) group within the [Islamic Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Front_\(Syria\)) criticised ISIL, saying: "They killed the people of Islam and leave the idol worshippers ... They use the verses talking about the disbelievers and implement it on the Muslims".[^cite_note-374] The main criticism of defectors from ISIL has been that the group is fighting and killing other Sunni Muslims,[^cite_note-375] as opposed to just non-Sunnis being brutalised.[^cite_note-376] In one case, a supposed defector from ISIL executed two activists of a Syrian opposition group in Turkey who had sheltered them.[^cite_note-377]&#x20;

### Other commentaries

Literature scholar [Ian Almond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ian_Almond) criticised the media commentators, the lack of balance in reporting, and the "way we are learning to talk about ISIS". While there was talk about 'radical evil' and 'radical Islam', Almond found it striking because "some of the most revered and oft-quoted figures in our Western political tradition have been capable of the most vicious acts of savagery—and yet all we ever hear about is how much the Middle East has to learn from us." Almond goes on to argue that [Winston Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill) "wanted to gas women and children", that [Ronald Reagan](25433.md)'s Central American policies "disembowelled more children than ISIS", that President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama)'s "planes and drones have dropped bombs on as many schoolchildren as ISIS", that former secretary of state [Madeleine Albright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madeleine_Albright) commented on the deaths of Iraqi children killed by sanctions, that [Henry Kissinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Kissinger) and [Margaret Thatcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margaret_Thatcher) "assisted in the torture and disappearance of thousands of Chilean students and labour activists... For anyone familiar with the history of both U.S. and European torture and murder over the past 150 years, it might not be all that hyperbolic to say that in ISIS, what we see more than anything else is a more expansive, explicit version of our own cruelties. In bombing ISIS and its would-be imperialism, we are really bombing a version of ourselves."[^cite_note-378]&#x20;

Commentator [Tom Engelhardt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Engelhardt) attributed the rise of ISIL and the destruction that followed to what he dubbed as America's drive to establish its own caliphate in the region.[^cite_note-379]&#x20;

A leader article in the *[New Scientist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Scientist)* magazine contextualised ISIL within the [nation state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nation_state) construct. Although the group is described as medieval in the pejorative sense, "it is also hyper-modern, interested in few of the trappings of a conventional state apart from its own brutal brand of law enforcement. In fact, it is more of a network than a nation, having made canny use of social media to exert influence far beyond its geographical base."[^cite_note-380]&#x20;

The [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) in its [Resolution 1267](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1267) (1999) described [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden) and his al-Qaeda associates as operators of a network of [terrorist training camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorist_training_camp).[^cite_note-419] The UN's [Al-Qaida Sanctions Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaida_and_Taliban_Sanctions_Committee) first listed ISIL in its Sanctions List under the name "Al-Qaida in Iraq" on 18 October 2004, as an entity/group associated with al-Qaeda. On 2 June 2014, the group was added to its listing under the name "Islamic State in Iraq and the Levant". The [European Union](9317.md) adopted the UN Sanctions List in 2002.[^cite_note-berghof-foundation.org-382]&#x20;



People lay flowers outside the [French embassy](6731624.md) in [Moscow](19004.md) in memory of the victims of the [November 2015 Paris attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks).

Many world leaders and government spokespeople have called ISIL a terrorist group or banned it, without their countries having formally designated it as such. The following are examples:&#x20;

The Government of Germany banned ISIL in September 2014. Activities banned include donations to the group, recruiting fighters, holding ISIL meetings and distributing its propaganda, [flying ISIL flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strafgesetzbuch_section_86a),[^cite_note-420] wearing ISIL symbols and all ISIL activities. "The terror organisation Islamic State is a threat to public safety in Germany as well", said German politician [Thomas de Maizière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_de_Maizi%C3%A8re). He added, "Today's ban is directed solely against terrorists who abuse religion for their criminal goals."[^cite_note-421] Being a member of ISIL is also illegal in accordance with § 129a and § 129b of the [German criminal code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strafgesetzbuch).[^cite_note-422]&#x20;

In October 2014, Switzerland banned ISIL's activities in the country, including propaganda and financial support of the fighters, with prison sentences as potential penalties.[^cite_note-423]&#x20;

In mid-December 2014, India banned ISIL after the arrest of an operator of a pro-ISIL Twitter account.[^cite_note-424]&#x20;

Pakistan designated ISIL as a banned organisation in late August 2015, under which all elements expressing sympathy for the group would be blacklisted and sanctioned.[^cite_note-ET-404]&#x20;

After its [2022 Ulema gathering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghanistan_Ulema_gathering), the [Taliban](30635.md) banned all Afghans from associating with the local [Khorasan Province](48029263.md) branch of IS in July 2022, and labelled it a "false sect".[^cite_note-Taliban-418]&#x20;

Media sources worldwide have described ISIL as a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-ISIS_or_ISIL?_The_debate-138][^cite_note-nytimes27Aug14-219][^cite_note-ft-20140617-278][^cite_note-McCoyTop-249][^cite_note-Indonesia-390][^cite_note-425]&#x20;

Following the D-ISIS Ministerial in June 2023, [Secretary Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_Blinken) announced Abdallah Makki Muslih al-Rufay'i and Abu Bakr ibn Muhammad ibn 'Ali al-Mainuki, as terrorists under Executive Order 13224.[^cite_note-426]&#x20;

#### Militia, cult, territorial authority, and other classifications

By 2014, ISIL was increasingly being viewed as a [militia](20622.md) in addition to a terrorist group and a [cult](1008588.md).[^cite_note-Time-427] As major Iraqi cities fell to ISIL in June 2014, Jessica Lewis, a former US Army intelligence officer at the [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War), described ISIL at that time as&#x20;

> not a terrorism problem anymore, \[but rather] an army on the move in Iraq and Syria, and they are taking terrain. They have shadow governments in and around [Baghdad](4492.md), and they have an aspirational goal to govern. I don't know whether they want to control Baghdad, or if they want to destroy the functions of the Iraqi state, but either way the outcome will be disastrous for Iraq.[^cite_note-Time-427]



Supporters of the Turkish Labour Party protesting in London following the [2015 Ankara bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Ankara_bombings)

Lewis has called ISIL:&#x20;

> An advanced military leadership. They have incredible command and control and they have a sophisticated reporting mechanism from the field that can relay tactics and directives up and down the line. They are well-financed, and they have big sources of manpower, not just the foreign fighters, but also prisoner escapees.[^cite_note-Time-427]

Former US Defense Secretary [Chuck Hagel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Hagel) saw an "imminent threat to every interest we have", but former top counter-terrorism adviser [Daniel Benjamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Benjamin) derided such talk as a "farce" that panics the public.[^cite_note-428]&#x20;

Writing for *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, [Pankaj Mishra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pankaj_Mishra) rejects the idea that the group is a resurgence of medieval Islam, saying instead:&#x20;

> In actuality, ISIS is the canniest of all traders in the flourishing international economy of disaffection: the most resourceful among all those who offer the security of collective identity to isolated and fearful individuals. It promises, along with others who retail racial, national and religious supremacy, to release the anxiety and frustrations of the private life into the violence of the global.[^cite_note-429]

On 28 January 2017, U.S. president Donald Trump issued a National Security Presidential Memorandum that called for a comprehensive plan to destroy ISIL to be formulated by the Defense Department within 30 days.[^cite_note-430]&#x20;

## Supporters

Main article: [Collaboration with the Islamic State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collaboration_with_the_Islamic_State)

A United Nations report from May 2015 showed that 25,000 "foreign terrorist fighters" from 100 countries had joined "Islamist" groups, many of them working for IS or al-Qaeda.[^cite_note-431]&#x20;

According to a June 2015 [Reuters](18998750.md) report that cited "jihadist ideologues" as a source, 90% of IS's fighters in Iraq were Iraqi, and 70% of its fighters in Syria were Syrian. The article stated that the group had 40,000 fighters and 60,000 supporters across its two primary strongholds in Iraq and Syria.[^cite_note-secret_of_Baghdadi's_success-103] According to scholar [Fawaz Gerges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fawaz_Gerges) writing in *ISIS: A History*, some "30 percent of the senior figures" in IS's military command were former army and police officers from the disbanded Iraqi security forces, turned towards Sunni Islamism and drawn to IS by the US [de-Ba'athification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De-Ba%27athification) policy following the [U.S. invasion of Iraq](201936.md).[^cite_note-NYRoB-7-9-2015-217]&#x20;

A 2014 analysis of 2,195,000 [Arabic-language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic) social media posts cited by *The Guardian* had 47% of the postings from Qatar, 35% from Pakistan, 31% from Belgium and almost 24% from the UK classified as supportive of IS.[^cite_note-432] According to a 2015 poll by [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center), Muslim populations of various Muslim-majority countries have overwhelmingly negative views of IS, with the highest percentage of those expressing favourable views not exceeding 14%.[^cite_note-433] In most of these countries, concerns about Islamic extremism have been growing.[^cite_note-434]&#x20;

There is a large amount of IS supporters online (On apps such as [Discord](49271581.md), [Telegram](41501305.md), among several other online forums).[^cite_note-435] In 2024, at least 10,000 IS fighters were still active in Syria, according to estimates by the Kurdish-led [SDF](48205248.md).[^cite_note-436] As of 2025, approximately 10,000 suspected IS fighters and their supporters were being held in Syrian detention centres, and tens of thousands of their wives and children were living in secure camps.[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438]&#x20;

In January 2026, a [northeastern Syria offensive](82106178.md) conducted by the [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md) against the Kurdish-led Syrian Democratic Forces (SDF) led to a significant security vacuum, resulting in the escape and mass transfer of thousands of Islamic State detainees. Following the SDF's withdrawal from key facilities such as the [al-Hawl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hawl) and [al-Shaddadi](39348357.md) prisons,[^cite_note-439] the U.S. military began relocating up to 9,000 detainees to Iraq to prevent a resurgence of IS, while Syrian government forces assumed control of several major detention centres, including al-Aqtan prison in Raqqa.[^cite_note-440]&#x20;

## Countries and groups at war with IS



A map of all state-based opponents of IS \
&#x20; [Combined Joint Task Force – Operation Inherent Resolve](44618350.md) \
&#x20; Non-CJTF State opponents \
&#x20; Territories held by IS at its late 2015 peak

IS's claims to territory have brought it into armed conflict with many governments, militias and other armed groups. International rejection of IS as a terrorist entity and rejection of its claim to even exist have placed it in conflict with countries around the world.&#x20;

### Global Coalition to Counter the Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant

See also: [International military intervention against the Islamic State § International coalitions against ISIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_military_intervention_against_the_Islamic_State#International_coalitions_against_ISIL), and [Combined Joint Task Force – Operation Inherent Resolve](44618350.md)



Airstrikes in Syria by 24 September 2014

US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) announcing the [death of Abu Bakr al-Baghdadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Abu_Bakr_al-Baghdadi) on 26 October 2019

The Global Coalition to Counter the Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant (ISIL), also referred to as the Counter-ISIL Coalition or Counter-DAESH Coalition,[^cite_note-441] is a US-led group of nations and [non-state actors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-state_actor) that have committed to "work together under a common, multifaceted, and long-term strategy to degrade and defeat ISIL/Daesh". According to a joint statement issued by 59 national governments and the European Union on 3 December 2014, participants in the Counter-ISIL Coalition are focused on multiple lines of effort:[^cite_note-Anti-ISIL-442]&#x20;

1. Supporting military operations, capacity building, and training;

2. Stopping the flow of foreign terrorist fighters;

3. Cutting off ISIL/Daesh's access to financing and funding;

4. Addressing associated humanitarian relief and crises; and

5. Exposing ISIL/Daesh's true nature (ideological delegitimisation).

[Operation Inherent Resolve](44120613.md) is the operational name given by the US to military operations against ISIL and Syrian al-Qaeda affiliates. [Combined Joint Task Force – Operation Inherent Resolve](44618350.md) (CJTF–OIR) is co-ordinating the military portion of the response. The [Arab League](52625.md), [European Union](9317.md), [NATO](21133.md), and [GCC](220950.md) are part of the Counter-ISIL Coalition:[^cite_note-Anti-ISIL-442] According to the Pentagon, by December 2017 over 80,000 ISIL fighters had been killed in Iraq and Syria by CJTF-OIR airstrikes.[^cite_note-once_promised-131] By then the coalition had flown over 170,000 sorties,[^cite_note-443] 75–80% of combat sorties were conducted by the military of the United States, with the other 20–25% by Australia, Canada, Denmark, France, Jordan, Belgium, the Netherlands, Saudi Arabia, Turkey, the United Arab Emirates, and the United Kingdom.[^cite_note-444] According to the UK-based monitoring group *Airwars*, the air strikes and artillery of US-led coalition killed as many as 6,000 civilians in Iraq and Syria by the end of 2017.[^cite_note-445]&#x20;

Lebanon, which the U.S. considers part of the Global Coalition, fought off several [incursions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Civil_War_spillover_in_Lebanon) by ISIL, with the largest engagements taking place from June 2014 to August 2017, when several thousand ISIL fighters invaded from Syria and occupied Lebanese territory. The U.S. and UK-backed [Lebanese Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Army) succeeded in repulsing this invasion, killing or capturing over 1,200 ISIL fighters in the process.[^cite_note-446]&#x20;

On 21 December 2019, over 33 Islamist militants were killed in Mali by French forces using attack helicopters, drones and ground troops, alongside the border with Mauritania, where an Al-Qaeda-linked group operates.[^cite_note-447]&#x20;

#### Al-Qaeda



Military situation in Syria as of February 2026:\
&#x20;

- &#x20; Controlled by [Syrian transitional government](79584988.md) (includes [TAF](30205.md))

  &#x20; Controlled by [Kurdish groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DAANES) ([SDF](48205248.md))

  &#x20; Controlled by [local Druze factions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Legal_Committee_in_Suwayda)

  &#x20; Controlled by [Israeli Defense Forces](39237.md)

  &#x20; Controlled by [Assadist](36629941.md) insurgents

  &#x20; Controlled by [the Islamic State](9087364.md) (IS)

\
(For a more detailed, interactive map, see [Template\:Syrian Civil War detailed map](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Syrian_Civil_War_detailed_map).)

[Al-Nusra Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nusra_Front) is a branch of [al-Qaeda](1921.md) operating in Syria. Al-Nusra has launched many attacks and [bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_bombings_during_the_Syrian_Civil_War), mostly against targets affiliated with or supportive of the Syrian government.[^cite_note-478] There have been media reports that many of al-Nusra's foreign fighters have left to join al-Baghdadi's ISIL.[^cite_note-479]&#x20;

In February 2014, after continued tensions, al-Qaeda publicly disavowed any relations with ISIL.[^cite_note-480] However, ISIL and al-Nusra Front still cooperate with each other occasionally when they fight against the Syrian government.[^cite_note-481]&#x20;

> The two groups \[ISIL and al-Nusra] share a nihilistic worldview, a loathing for modernity, and for the West. They subscribe to the same perverted interpretations of Islam. Other common traits include a penchant for suicide attacks, and sophisticated exploitation of the internet and social media. Like ISIL, several Al Qaeda franchises are interested in taking and holding territory; AQAP has been much less successful at it. The main differences between Al Qaeda and ISIL are largely political—and personal. Over the past decade, Al Qaeda has twice embraced ISIL (and its previous manifestations) as brothers-in-arms.

— Bobby Ghosh, "ISIL and Al Qaeda: Terror's frenemies", *[Quartz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quartz_\(publication\))*[^cite_note-482]



Military Situation in Iraq in May 2020

On 10 September 2015, an audio message was released by al-Qaeda's leader [Ayman al-Zawahiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayman_al-Zawahiri) criticising ISIL's self-proclaimed caliphate and accusing it of "sedition". This was described by some media outlets as a "declaration of war".[^cite_note-483] However, although al-Zawahiri denied ISIL's legitimacy, he suggested that there was still room for cooperation against common enemies, and said that if he were in Iraq, he would fight alongside ISIL.[^cite_note-484]&#x20;

## Human rights abuse and war crime findings

Main article: [Human rights in Islamic State-controlled territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Islamic_State-controlled_territory)

See also: [Genocide of Yazidis by the Islamic State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_of_Yazidis_by_the_Islamic_State), [Persecution of Shias by the Islamic State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Shias_by_the_Islamic_State), [Persecution of Christians by the Islamic State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Christians_by_the_Islamic_State), and [Mass executions in Islamic State-occupied Mosul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_executions_in_Islamic_State-occupied_Mosul)

The Islamic State has committed or been widely accused of committing [war crimes](48626.md), [crimes against humanity](61099.md), and of [human rights abuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_abuses),[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486][^cite_note-Larson-487] including systematic rape and other forms of sexual violence against both males and females,[^cite_note-488][^cite_note-489] mass killing of [prisoners of war](25008.md),[^cite_note-490] [summary executions](1227309.md) including the [Camp Speicher massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_Speicher_massacre), public floggings, beheadings, politically motivated assassinations of judges; public officials; members of the security forces and others, and terrorising residents of [Derna, Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_ISIL_takeover_of_Derna) among others.[^cite_note-sarah-491] ISIL members were also reported to perform [human sacrifices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sacrifice),[^cite_note-492] despite the act being forbidden in Islam.[^cite_note-493] Sarah Leah Watson, Director of HRW Middle East and North Africa, said: "Commanders should understand that they may face domestic or international prosecution for the grave rights abuses their forces are committing."[^cite_note-sarah-491]&#x20;

In July 2014, the [BBC](19344654.md) reported the United Nations' chief investigator as stating: "Fighters from the Islamic State in Iraq and the Levant (ISIL) may be added to a list of war crimes suspects in Syria."[^cite_note-494]&#x20;

The [United Nations Commission on Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Commission_on_Human_Rights) has stated that the group "seeks to subjugate civilians under its control and dominate every aspect of their lives through terror, indoctrination, and the provision of services to those who obey".[^cite_note-UNRuleOfTerror-247]&#x20;

According to the [Iraq Body Count project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq_Body_Count_project), the Islamic State's fighters killed a minimum of 25,645 Iraqi civilians from 2014 to 2016.[^cite_note-495]&#x20;

The Islamic State's founder, Mr. Baghdadi, grafted two elements onto his Wahhabi foundations borrowed from the broader, 20th-century Islamist movements that began with the Muslim Brotherhood and ultimately produced Al Qaeda. Where Wahhabi scholars preach obedience to earthly rulers, Mr. Baghdadi adopted the call to political action against foreign domination of the Arab world that has animated the Muslim Brotherhood, Al Qaeda and other 20th-century Islamist movements.Mr. Baghdadi also borrowed the idea of a restored caliphate. Where Wahhabism first flourished alongside the Ottoman Caliphate, the Muslim Brotherhood was founded shortly after that caliphate's dissolution, in 1924—an event seen across the world as a marker of Western ascent and Eastern decline. The movement's founders took up the call for a revived caliphate as a goal of its broader anti-Western project.

[^cite_note-363]: &#x20;Creswell, Robyn; Haykel, Bernard (8 June 2015). ["Battle Lines"](https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/2015/06/08/battle-lines-jihad-creswell-and-haykel). *The New Yorker*. In the most recent issue of Dabiq, ISIS's English-language magazine, a female writer encourages women to emigrate to "the lands of the Islamic State" even if it means travelling without a male companion, a shocking breach of traditional Islamic law. This may be a cynical ploy—a lure for runaways. But it is in keeping with the jihadists' attack on parental authority and its emphasis on individual empowerment, including the power of female believers to renounce families they do not view as authentically Muslim.

[^cite_note-364]: &#x20;Creswell, Robyn; Haykel, Bernard (8 June 2015). ["Battle Lines"](https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/2015/06/08/battle-lines-jihad-creswell-and-haykel). *The New Yorker*. It has also created a female morality police, a shadowy group called the al-Khansa' Brigades, who insure proper deportment in ISIS-held towns. ... Al-Khansa' was a female poet of the pre-Islamic era who converted to Islam and became a companion of the Prophet, and her elegies for her male relations are keystones of the genre \[of Islamic poetry]. The name therefore suggests an institution with deep roots in the past, and yet there has never been anything like the Brigades in Islamic history, nor do they have an equivalent anywhere else in the Arab world.
